## Miami and Florida Farm

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracārine*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this day of your appearance, we are praying to Kṛṣṇa to allow us to become your personal servants. Just as when you were present on this planet your personal assistant cooked for you, cleaned for you, and in other ways assisted you in a very intimate way, we are wishing to do the same. We understand that it is possible because you are still personally present in the form of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and the instructions that you have given to each of your disciples to inform them of their personal service to you, which continues even after your disappearance.

Because ISKCON is your society, your transcendental body, to neglect ISKCON or to leave ISKCON is nondifferent from neglecting or leaving you. Just as when you were present on this planet there were some rascals who knowingly left your association, thinking they could carry on their spiritual lives without you, now there are fools who think that their spiritual lives can go on outside of ISKCON. As you state in your books, “They are simply living a hallucination.” Because their desire is more important in their minds than your desire, they leave to carry out these desires, which, as we know from your *Bhagavad-gītā,* can never be fulfilled.

If somehow or other we feel ourselves being chastised by ISKCON, we should accept this as your chastisement. The spiritual master’s duty is to chastise his disciple in order to bring him to the correct understanding of devotional service. The disciple should never reject the spiritual master for this chastisement, but should appreciate the love which this chastisement represents, for the spiritual master is taking the hard responsibility of training up his spiritual sons and daughters. ISKCON’s chastisement is your chastisement, and should only increase our surrender to your lotus feet. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu prayed to Kṛṣṇa that Kṛṣṇa may crush Him by His embrace or leave Him brokenhearted by not being present before Him, but still Kṛṣṇa would remain His worshipable Lord. This should also be our attitude towards you and your mission, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

Now that you are no longer physically present on this planet, it is necessary that we become your first-class servants, First-class servant means one who does not have to be told what to do for the master, but knows the master’s mentality so well that he automatically carries out the master’s desires. The second-class servant carries out the master’s orders after being informed of them. It is essential that all of your disciples and granddisciples come to the first-class platform; but to come to this platform it is essential that they understand your mentality and your particular desire for them in regard to their personal service to Kṛṣṇa. Everyone is given a particular service by the spiritual master and a particular place to execute this service, as well as devotees to work with or under.

Your personal instructions to us are still ringing in our ears. Every day we hear you repeat them over and over again. You stated, “You should never think that there is any better way of preaching than book distribution.” Also you told us, “You should always cooperate with Hṛdayānanda Mahārāja.” We are tightly clinging to your instructions, just as a man in the ocean clings to his life preserver.

So the instructions and realizations given to us by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are our only possessions in this material world, and we are praying that we become their servant, and not fall prey to our tendency, as you have stated, “to always want to change things.”

*Your servants at ISKCON Miami and Kṛṣṇaworld (Florida Farm),*

## Middle East

**Golden Dawn**

Alas, though drowning deep in  
Waves of colored hue,  
Raging winds and thrashing gale  
Doth blight all reason true.  
Tongues offire lickingflesh,  
Hell in all directions bound,  
Scores of fallen angels bleeding  
Stains upon the ground.  
Madness, death and courting friends  
Do rule this land below.  
The nether regions manifest  
Upon unsaintly brow.  
No hope, no chance, no remedy,  
Salvation fled in fear,  
Age-old human comedy’s  
Familiar laughing sneer.  
And rows of dead, rising up  
To punishment deserved,  
Just to fall again to dust:  
Only Mammon served.  
Skull ‘n bones, a cold gravestone  
Commemorating life,  
Ancient mysteries lying deep  
‘Neath layers of human strife.  
Nescience—lord amongst the fools,  
Crippled, blind and lame,  
Crawling, groping, stumbling selves,  
Though naked, without shame.  
Amidst this night of dread and fear  
Our mindful Lord did send  
A rare and precious jewel of light,  
All broken dreams to mend.  
Prabhupāda—the Lord’s sweet wish  
To ignite the darkened soul,  
Pouring immortal nectar sweet,  
All cups to overflow.  
Our vessels full and lips still moist  
Shall we now begin our task  
To spread his mercy everywhere  
All souls rejoice at last!

## Monterrey

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam states, *yasya sādkṣād bhagavati jñāna-dīpa-prade gurau:* “The spiritual master should be considered to be directly the Supreme Lord because he gives transcendental knowledge for enlightenment.” *(Bhāg.* 7.15.26)

It is recommended that one honor the spiritual master as being on equal status with the Supreme Personality of Godhead: *sāksād-dharitvena samasta-śāstraih.* This is enjoined in every scripture: *ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyāt.*

One should consider the *ācārya* to be as good as the Supreme Personality of Godhead. The spiritual master is called *sevaka-bhagavān,* the servitor Personality of Godhead, who is to be worshiped. The spiritual master is worshiper God, whereas the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, is the worshipable God. This is the difference between the spiritual master and the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Another point: *Bhagavad-gītā,* which constitutes the instructions of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, is presented by the spiritual master *as it* is, without deviation. Therefore the Absolute Truth is present in the spiritual master. As clearly stated, *jñāna-dīpa-prade.* The Supreme Personality of Godhead gives real knowledge to the entire world, and the spiritual master, as the representative of the Supreme Godhead, carries the message throughout the world. Therefore, on the absolute platform, there is no difference between the spiritual master and the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Dear master, kindly enlighten us in transcendental knowledge, which may act as a torchlight by which we may cross the dark nescience of material existence.

My dear lord, to be engaged always in thinking of your lotus feet is very difficult. It is possible for a devotee who has already achieved love for you and is engaged in transcendental loving service. My dear lord, I wish that my mind also may be engaged somehow or other in meditating on your lotus feet, at least for some time.

My dear lord, I am a living entity perpetually disturbed by the conditions of material existence, and I have been broken into different pieces by the smashing wheel of material existence. Because of my various sinful activities while existing in this material world, I am burning in the blazing fire of material reactions. Somehow or other, my dear lord, I have come to take shelter under your lotus feet. Please accept me and give me protection.

O all-merciful spiritual master, representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, when will my mind be completely surrendered unto your lotus feet? At that time, only by your mercy, I shall be able to get relief from all obstacles to spiritual life, and I shall be situated in blissful life.

Your servants in Monterrey,

Vrajendra-kumāra dāsa brahmacārī / Rāma-svarūpa dāsa brahmacārī / Hare Kṛṣṇa dāsa brahmacārī / Jīvana-Kṛṣṇa dāsa brahmacārī / Sadāśiva dāsa brahmacārī / Vrajabhūmi dāsa brahmacārī / Caitanya-deva dāsa brahmacārī / Acyutānanda dāsa brahmacārī / Kumuda dāsa brahmacārī / Rāma-Govinda dāsa adhikārī / Advaya-jñāna dāsa adhikārī / Lakṣmī- Nārāyaṇa dāsa adhikārī / Bhakta Eduardo / Bhakta Rafael / Bhakta Esteban / Girisutā-devī dāsī / Gandhinī-devī dāsī / Satyavatī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Eva / Bhaktin Magdalena / Rādhā-Govinda / Varīyān / Rūpa-mañjarī

## Nairobi

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. In *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*it is described how Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, while passing through the forest of Jhārikhaṇḍa, was able to distribute love of God to the animals. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you also went into a junglelike existence in the Lower East Side of New York City, and you also released so many conditioned souls from animallike existence.

Preaching in East Africa, we feet ourselves to be somewhere between the Jhārikhaṇḍa forest and the Lower East Side. The jungle forests of Kenya are on our doorstep, while in the city of Nairobi there breeds the uncontrollable lust of a million souls struggling to maintain their existence. Out of your causeless compassion to deliver the planet from illusion, Your Divine Grace not only came to Africa to preach and enact your transcendental pastimes, but you also established Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vaṅka-vihārī to give wonderful solace to the exhausted minds of the materialists. Were it not for Their Lordships, we doubt if any of us would be able to continue here. This is yet another of Your Divine Grace’s expert plans to keep us engaged in devotional service.

While we struggle on in our humble way, trying to spread this *saṅkīrtana* movement, preaching to whomever will listen, giving books to whomever can read and engaging whomever can do some service, we recall the words of Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī:

*kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye 'nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*

**“Kirāta, Hūṇa, Āndhra, Pulinda, Pulkaśa, Ābhīra, Śumbha, Yavana, members of the Khasa races and even others addicted to sinful acts can be purified by taking shelter of the devotees of the Lord, due to His being the supreme power. I beg to offer my respectful obeisances unto Him.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us all shelter at your lotus feet.

*The devotees of Nairobi Yātrā,*

## Newark

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The vedic literature states, *mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ:* for spiritual advancement one must follow in the footsteps of previous authorities. Except for the completely independent Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, everyone is following some authority, material or spiritual. A spirit soul absorbed in a material bodily conception of existence is under the jurisdiction of material nature. Forgetful of his relationship with the Supreme Lord, the conditioned soul blindly serves the whips of lust, anger, greed, illusion, and words in activities of sensory gratification. Struggling to control and enjoy material nature, he is aware of neither the degree of his material conditioning nor the power of the actual controller and enjoyer, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

The spiritual authorities are Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and those who represent Him in disciplic succession. Lord Kṛṣṇa, the controller and source of all material and spiritual energies, is truly qualified to be worshiped by everyone. In *Bhagavad-gītā* Lord Kṛṣṇa has advised everyone to surrender unto Him and thus become happy and free from all material distress. Lord Kṛṣṇa created the material world to give the rebellious souls an opportunity to surrender unto Him; therefore, He says that no one is dearer to Him than one who distributes the knowledge of *bhakti-yoga,* devotional service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the disciplic representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead who has spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, you are most dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Your accomplishments in the Lord’s devotional service are too vast to measure—the opening of over one hundred centers, translating over seventy major works of Vaiṣṇava literature, accepting over forty-five hundred disciples and traveling to Rāvaṇa’s kingdom in the Western world, at the age of seventy, to begin a massive assault on impersonalism and godlessness. You have thus spread an effulgence of Kṛṣṇa consciousness by your every word, thought and action—and by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s grace this effulgence continues to shine brightly.

We can only pray to be engaged in your devotional service and to become a pure instrument, assisting you in fulfilling the desires of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

*Your servants in Newark,*

Prahlādānanda Swami / Amṛtāṁśa dāsa / Palāyita dāsa / Kṛṣṇa-nāma dāsa

## New Barsana

ISKCON New Barsana Farm Community would like to humbly make this offering at the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda—

You are our hope, our life, and our soul.  
You’ve lifted us out of māyā’s dark hole.  
And all that you’ve asked us is that we extol  
Kṛṣṇa, and make Him our life’s only goal.  
Try as we might, in this life or another,  
We can never repay this transcendental offer.  
We have no means to humbly proffer.  
You’re a spiritual king, and we are all paupers.  
If we possess any good qualities  
It’s only because you’ve given us these.  
But we’ll try our best your burden to ease  
By preaching your message with great expertise.  
With the strength of a lion, the touch of a dove,  
You gave us this knowledge sent from above.  
Your mercy is all that we are made of,  
We have but one thing we can give you—our love.  
And so, Śrīla Prabhupāda, intensely we pray  
For the strength and the faith, your will to obey.  
Thus fixed at your lotus feet we will all stay.  
Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, don’t kick us away.

Your servants at New Barsana

## New Delhi

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please let me offer my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the true representative of Lord Kṛṣṇa. That is why you could present *Bhagavad-gītā* as it is. After your departure, *Bhagavad-gītā* has now been published even in Arabic and Chinese, and hundreds and thousands of people are becoming devotees by reading this presentation.

In the beginning, when I joined, I thought that you would be like any other *sādhu* in India, but after hearing you I could realize that you were different. I joined in 1972 and was brought in contact with your lotus feet. Then I had many opportunities to hear your discourses at our temple in Juhu in Bombay. There I heard you using terms like *fools, rascals, cats, dogs,* etc. Nice, well-dressed, elderly, big and rich, handsome—very many people would come to hear you. But you would often tell them that they were fools, rascals, cats and dogs. I would contemplate, and wonder what kind of *sādhu is* this? Nice, nice people were coming to listen to him, and he is calling them by all these names! Cannot he use some other, nice and better terms? The kind of remarks made by you, I thought, were unusual and unexpected. I had very little appreciation for them all. To begin with, I was in darkness, but when some more time passed, mercifully you injected some purity in me. Then, with your blessings, I was able to begin to see what is what and who is who. To some extent I began realizing that, yes, it was true that these people were fools and no less than cats and dogs. Later on, I also started using these terms during my preaching, where I was repeating your message. While engaged in this preaching mission, more and more I was realizing people’s actual fallen position and your true position as the eternal, truthful representative of the Lord.

Flattering each other has become so common that even *sādhus* are seen flattering their disciples and the audience. Otherwise, there is the risk of not getting enough followers and enough *dakṣinā* (the cash in hand). But you seemed not to be worried about that. You were worried about actually waking them up, helping them realize who they were. In the presence of the spiritual master, one must feel as very fallen, fool number one. You were trying to give them this understanding, and if they would accept themselves as fools then immediately their spiritual life would begin. Māyāvāda philosophy has spread all over so much, and it has mixed with the mind and blood of the people. This Māyāvāda philosophy advocates the goal as becoming free from all kinds of feelings, including the feeling of anger. As one gets into exhibiting so-called good qualities of not getting angry at others, he ends up also not being a true representative of the Lord.

Lord Kṛṣṇa exhibited these feelings of anger when he was talking to Śrī Arjuna. Kṛṣṇa chastises Arjuna very heavily for posing himself as a very learned person. He does not talk as if everything is all right or as if it is all one. Instead, He discriminates and states very clearly that He comes to protect His devotees and also to annihilate demons. He also says,

*na māṁ duṣkṛtino mūḍhāḥ*  
*prapadyante narādhamāḥ*  
*māyayāpahṛta-jñānā*  
*āsuraṁ bhāvam āśritāḥ*

**“Those miscreants who are grossly foolish, lowest among mankind, whose knowledge is stolen by illusion, and who partake of the atheistic nature of demons, do not surrender unto Me.” (Bg. 7.15)**

These four kinds of miscreants do not surrender to Him. He also says, *avajānanti māṁ mūdhā mānuṣīṁ tanum āśritam:* “Fools deride Me when I descend in the human form.” (Bg. 9.11) They are fools. So Lord Kṛṣṇa was not shy using these terms. And you, being very, very loyal to Lord Kṛṣṇa, were representing His message *as it is*. Thus your presentation was befitting, and not Māyāvādī-like.

When I came to your lotus feet I had naturally so many misconceptions, and the sample one is as mentioned above. I am maintaining these contaminated conceptions to different degrees, which is offensive to you, directly or indirectly. I liked you from the very beginning. At the same time, I was very, very offensive at your feet. This offensive mentality is diminished, but it is not finished. Help me; bless me to realize your true exalted position, so that I may stop being offensive to you altogether. I know I cannot please Lord Kṛṣṇa without stopping committing offenses at your feet. And I know I have committed very many of them, and am still continuing them. Please help me to put a full stop to all this, once and for all. If this humble Vyāsa-pūjā offering of mine is published, you will of course read it, but if anyone other than you happens to read, they may wonder about the point that I am attempting to make. They may or may not understand it, but I know for sure you would know what I mean. You know everything. You have known me since the beginning. You knew my thoughts in the beginning. You know them even today, and you will always know them in the future. You knew that I was offensive but you are so kind that you just tolerated it. I have tried to express these hidden offensive feelings inside me, but you have always been aware of these. Now I am taking advantage of this Vyāsa-pūjā offering time and trying to cleanse out these dirty things in a formal way. That’s all. I feel very, very guilty for not realizing your true position when I joined, and even up to date. I do not have the full realization that you are a *paramahaṁsa* devotee, swanlike, spotlessly clean, with no material tinge in your personality. You are a Vaikuṇṭha man. You are Kṛṣṇa’s man, not ordinary. God realization, or Kṛṣṇa realization, is only possible when I realize your position as Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead. I am begging you to help me realize this, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

You will see in the other Vyāsa-pūjā offerings in this book that your children are all very nicely glorifying you. But I have no capacity to do that. I am just an offender. An offender cannot glorify you. Please help me to get rid of this offensive mentality so that I can begin to say a few words about you, the glorious one, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Humbly and lonely, begging for your immediate reciprocation in this regard,

Your unworthy servants,

Lokanātha Swami / Gaurasundara dāsa / Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Hari-bhakti-vilāsa dāsa / Ekanātha dāsa / Vijaya dāsa / Madana-mohana dāsa / Ādityeśa dāsa / Jagannātha-suta dāsa / Tulasī dāsa / Yadu-kuleśvara dāsa / Prāṇa Govinda dāsa / Karṇāmṛta dāsa / Candraśekhara dāsa / Kṛṣṇa Gopāla dāsa / Śrī Rāma dāsa / Dāmodara dāsa / Ānandamaya dāsa / Puruṣottama dāsa / Kuñja-vihārī dāsa / Bhakta Shinde / Bhakta Hari Charan / Bhakta Rādhā Krishan / Bhakta Prabhat / Bhakta Ram Kumar / Bhakta Arun / Bhakta Sinha / Bhakta B.R. Malik / Bhakta Bhu Dev / Bhakta Vijay / Bhakta Prakash / Bhakta Vithal / Bhakta Srimanta / Rādhā-parṣatī-devī dāsī / Premaka-devī dāsī / Nirmala-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Sheela

## New Orleans

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories unto you! You are the savior of the fallen souls of this dark age of Kali. The mercy which you have bestowed upon us is like the never-ending oceans of transcendental milk flowing from the innumerable milk cows of the spiritual world, *kṣīrābdhiḥ sravati surabhibhyaḥ.*

We have seen your supreme power of preaching, and so doing within a few short years you have changed the face of the earth. The *mahā-mantra* is deeply imbedded into the consciousness of almost everyone by your preaching, and it is growing like never before. Kṛṣṇa’s name and the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement are so well known that all forms of the media, such as radio, newspapers, television, movies, etc., constantly display different aspects of the sublime message you have so kindly delivered.

If someone should try to halt this epidemic, he will fail. It is simply too late. Everywhere this consciousness is expanding, like a giant lotus flower. Your books can be found in practically every nook and corner. You must be so pleased to see this. Our desire is to assist you eternally in the mission of Lord Caitanya.

Because of you, now every person can know Śrī Vṛndāvana. Therefore it is completely fitting to say that you are the transcendental purpose tree—*kalpa-taru.* You are the desire-fulfilling gem—*cintāmaṇi-gaṇamayī***—**and you are a spiritual river of nectar, from which anyone can drink—*toyam amṛtam.* Your walking is a beautiful dance—*naṭyaṁ gamanam***—**and your enlightening speech is a spiritual song—*kathā gānam.*

Under these conditions of unlimited good fortune, now the whole universe should bow down at your lotus feet again and again. Still, only a small number of them have recognized the treasures you have given. *Golokam iti yaṁ vidantas te santaḥ kṣiti-virala-cārāḥ katipayāḥ.* That realm is known as Goloka only to a very few self-realized souls in this world.

Therefore, please give us one drop of your mercy. We wish to please you by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious and spreading this consciousness everywhere, eternally in your service.

The devotees of Saṇkīrtana-pura, New Orleans

## New York

“Prabhupada, where can there be another center in New York?”

“East side ... West side ... All around the town.”

And then you expanded, brought down the spiritual sky, and planted the seeds of the tree of Lord Caitanya, which grew like the mango tree that Mahāprabhu made blossom and bear fruit in moments.

When we first saw you we couldn’t tell if it was your exquisite form that beguiled us or the spirit within it. Now we know that they were one and the same. Then you charmed away all we had (some wrongly still feel cheated). But it was not for yourself, but for Kṛṣṇa; and it was never ours, but always His. Divine slavery has made us freer than the chained kings of the world.

*Your servants at Rādhā-Govinda Mandira,*

## North Florida

*je ānilo prema-dhana koruṇā pracur*  
*heno prabhu kothā gelā ācārya-ṭhākur*  
*kāhā mora swarūp rūpa kāhā sanātan*  
*kāhā dāsa raghunātha patita-pāvan*  
*kāhā mora bhaṭṭa-juga kāhā kavirāj*  
*eka-kāle kothā gelā gorā naṭa-rāj*  
*pāṣāṇe kuṭibo māthā anale paśibo*  
*gaurāṅga guṇera nidhi kothā gele pābo*  
*se-saba saṅgīra saṅge je koilo bilās*  
*se-saṅga nā pāiyā kānde narottama dās*

**He who brought the treasure of divine love and who was filled with compassion and mercy-where has such a personality as Śrīnivāsa Ācārya gone? Where are my Svarūpa Dāmodara and Rūpa Gosvāmī? Where is Sanātana? Where is Raghunātha dāsa, the savior of the fallen? Where are my Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa and Gopāla Bhaṭṭa, and where is Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja? Where did Lord Gaurāṅga, the great dancer, suddenly go? I will smash my head against the rock and enter into the fire. Where will I find Lord Gaurāṅga, the reservoir of all wonderful qualities? Being unable to obtain the association of Lord Gaurāṅga accompanied by all of these devotees in whose association He performed His pastimes, Narottama dāsa simply weeps.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. I remember when the announcement was made early in the morning that you had departed for the spiritual world. At first it did not even register. The thought of not seeing you any more was completely unthinkable. You had been ill for a long time, but you had promised to stay and finish the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* The fact that you had actually left started to sink in only hours, days and even years later. I was in Hawaii at that time, and I realized with tears in my eyes then and now, that no longer would there be the ecstatic *kīrtana* greeting at the Honolulu Airport; the innumerable tropical flower garlands stacking up on your shoulders, till they had to be removed; the rip-roaring *guru-pūjā āratis* where you would shower flowers on all the devotees. No longer would I be able to garland you with fragrant flowers or apply the sandalwood paste to your forehead and temples; no more morning walks on Hawaii’s world-famous beaches; no longer would I be able to watch you pay your most respectful obeisances to the Pañca-tattva, or touch your lotus feet, or offer you a big plate of fruit and devour the remnants. No longer would I hear you translating *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in the very early morning. No more *mahā* plates of yours (with pineapple pulp and eggplant skins) to honor. No more walks to the departing gate for your flight to the West Coast. Now I can only meditate on these fond memories of you and beat my head on the rocks in separation.

Of course, Your Divine Grace has been so kind to leave behind your books, your taped classes, and numerous films that I can also take shelter of and meditate upon. You also have actually fully manifested yourself on the pages of Satsvarūpa Mahārāja’s wonderful book *Śrīla Prabhupāda-liīlāmṛta.*

And of course, by following your instructions to chant sixteen rounds, follow the four regulative principles, rise early, bathe, attend the morning and evening programs, and especially by following your instruction to preach and distribute your books, I will always be with you. So on this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, please accept our humble obeisances and prayers; shower your mercy and blessings upon us. Give us the potency, the intelligence, the determination and the desire to assist you in your great mission to save the world.

Allow us to be with you. If you examine us you will find no qualities. Your mercy is all that we are made of. If you are not merciful unto us, we can only weep and will not be able to maintain our lives.

*Begging to become your eternal servants,*

ISKCON of North Florida

## Ottawa

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ*  
*paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande*  
*śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of the fallen souls. His mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains. I must explain the glory of my spiritual master, who alone is causing the transformation of the world.”**

We offer all glories to you, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, our most beloved spiritual master.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are without any doubt a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra,* an empowered servant of the Lord coming directly from the spiritual world. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were sent by the Supreme Lord on a mission to deliver the fallen souls of this universe. We, with our tiny brains, find it difficult to understand how you have been sent on similar missions to other universes. We can attempt to appreciate your inconceivable love for Lord Kṛṣṇa by recollecting all of the sublime and nectarean pastimes you manifested amongst us in this world. However, since our vision is very limited, we cannot properly glorify such a powerful personality as you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but we are moved to offer a meager appreciation of your unlimited qualities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how can we misfits properly glorify such a divine personality as you?  
We are aware there’s no one who compares to Your Divine Grace, ‘tis true.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, throughout the universe you’re glorified as a reservoir of propitious qualities.  
Ah! But what are we, a far-stretching maelstrom of desultory and malign personalities?  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, for many years in India you primed painstakingly for your momentous preaching.  
You meditated on giving the West’s spiritual desert the oasis of Lord Caitanya’s teaching.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, when young people were just beginning to seek answers, you set sail.  
You gallantly journeyed upon the Jaladuta; despite two heart attacks, your determinaption didn’t fail.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you, by Kṛṣṇa’s grace, cruised the boundless Atlantic  
Ocean to its border.  
You came to the hellish Western world because that was your guru’s solemn order.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you disembarked finding almost everyone suffering in the jaws of material nature.  
You arrived at the eleventh hour with the holy name—’tis the only cure.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, seventeen years later, we humbly try to uphold what  
you created.  
You generated an ocean of devotion, by which the world would soon be  
inundated.

*Your fallen children and grandchildren at New Kulinagrama Dhāma,*

## Perth

Śrīla Prabhupāda said, “The books should go out,”  
And those with great faith would have no doubt  
That by doing this, the world will find out  
Their greatest friend, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Now, at first it may appear as only a dream—  
Everybody Kṛṣṇa conscious? That degraded scene?  
But within his instructions, the potency keen.  
Empowered preacher, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Godbrothers not caring, only fighting in court,  
Prabhupāda pushed on alone, printing the sort  
Of books for the future, which will be eagerly sought  
Bythe followers of Prabhupāda.  
He struggled tirelessly for so many years,  
Chanting, and writing with blood, sweat and tears  
of ecstasy, so that one day the ears  
**of the fallen would hear Kṛṣṇa. Jaya Prabhupāda.**  
His mother and father of Vaiṣṇava descent  
Prayed to Rādhārāṇī for blessings God-sent.  
Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta was fully content  
With such a disciple as Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Abhay Charan De was his childhood name,  
And Moti, Kachora-mukhi increased his fame  
To the day when we uncontrolled, whom he would tame,  
Would request, “May we call you Prabhupāda?”  
Who can count the glories of our Gurudeva?  
Embodiment of mercy, with a tidal wave  
Of love of Godhead; his intention to save  
The poor jīvas, that’s why, “Prabhupāda.  
Prabhupāda is our father, ‘cause he planted the seed  
Of devotional service, our only need;  
Mother, through the milk of instructions decreed  
Our life and our soul, Prabhupāda.  
His twin feet we worship as the goal of life.  
He renounced all possessions, children and wife  
For a spiritual family, where desire was rife  
To please, love and serve (our) Prabhupāda.  
His voice was like thunder and like a rose, too;  
His lotuslike eyes and soft golden hue  
Were the idol of Vaiṣṇavas pure and true.  
That’s why we called him Prabhupāda!  
We would sit at his feet, and those lucky, massage;  
Like Kṛṣṇa, he had a personal entourage.  
His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Mahārāja  
Prabhupāda, our dear Prabhupāda.  
Hardly caring to eat or sleep, he would work  
In Paris, Australia, London or New York.  
Avyabhicāri-bhakti—translating the books  
For Kali-yuga. Patita-pāvana Prabhupāda.  
At the Lord’s feet he is firmly fixed;  
His devotion to Kṛṣṇa was never mixed  
With karma, jñāna or yoga, māyā’s subtle tricks.  
Śuddha-bhakti! said our Prabhupāda.  
His Guru Mahārāja’s yardstick for success  
Was how many times the word Kṛṣṇa was stressed.  
As his servants we tend to think that the best  
Is to stress the success of Prabhupāda.  
The spotless Purāṇa from Sri Suka’s face  
And Caitanya-caritāmṛta by Kavirāja’s grace  
Are sublime; but now Līlāmṛta must take first place,  
Because it’s about Prabhupāda.  
So now is the time to put differences aside,  
Allow all ill-feeling and party spirit to subside.  
Cast off the effects of Kali-yuga’s tide  
By cooperating for Prabhupāda.  
Then our love for him will surely be proved  
And the influence of anger and envy removed,  
So that day fast approaches when all are imbued  
With love of the “Greatest”—Prabhupāda.

*Your sons and daughters of Perth Temple,*

## Philadelphia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracārine*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**A Poem to His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Again in homes and āśramas, maṭhas and temples,  
The ārati lamps are lit, kīrtana resounds,  
Strung flowers, petals flung, a feast is offered  
To honor you, whose praise can have no bounds.  
Whose words do we repeat? Who guides our pen?  
As yogis fill their cups and empty them  
To worship Gaṅgā-mā with her own water,  
We sing the song you’ve sung us back again.  
Who dares, when disregarding your instructions,  
To call himself “ācārya” or “guru”?  
If Western men know anything of Kṛṣṇa,  
Without a doubt we’ve learned it all ftom you.  
The spirit soul, prakṛti, time and karma,  
The cautionary tales of māyā’s hooks,  
Sambandha, abhidheya, and prayojana,  
Yourself and Kṛṣṇa—all are in your books.  
Beloved father, kindly bless your children  
And theirs, so that, before it is too late,  
We leave aside our impure motivations  
And, doing as you asked, cooperate.  
Then, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with love and trust,  
Remembering you, who gave us everything,  
We’ll read and sell your books and humbly give  
Our lives as your Vyāsa-pūjā offering.

Your servants at Philadelphia ISKCON,

## Portugal

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

It isearly afternoon here in Portugal, and not ten minutes have passed since I escorted a middle-aged couple from Lisbon to the door of our new preaching center here. It wasn’t a highly unusual meeting, but in light of the little turbulence that has risen in your movement, I found the encounter to be very inspiring. Our center had not been open one week, and this couple was the first to arrive at our door with one of your priceless gifts to the world, *Easy Journey to Other Planets.* They had received the book in the parking lot of a local department store, and after reading the book in two days were extremely eager to know more about the philosophy contained within it. After discussing for twenty minutes the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, their questions changed in nature and they began asking all about you as the author. Who is this man? Will he ever come to Portugal? I had to explain to them that you had left this world to return to Kṛṣṇa, but that you had not done so before carefully making all the necessary arrangements to ensure the Kṛṣṇa consciousness of millions of people for countless generations to come. They were a little disheartened to know that they missed the opportunity of seeing you personally, but became encouraged as I explained how you had struggled for eighty years to make people like them fortunate and happy, and that although they could not see you physically, the fruits of your struggle and lifetime of service to Kṛṣṇa were still available to them in the form of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Later we exchanged farewells, and they left with another book, *Elevation to Kṛṣṇa Consciousness* and avowedly promising to attend our first Sunday feast the following week.

I sat down and began to seriously reflect on how carefully you had indeed arranged for the world’s progressive development in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and ultimate liberation from the cycle of birth and death. We can only begin to consider how fortunate we have been made by your persistence, patience, and ardent determination to give us Kṛṣṇa consciousness in spite of ourselves.

You always quoted Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s saying, “Don’t try to see God; simply act so as God will want to see you.” What to speak of *mādhurya-rasa,* the simple realization “I am not this body; I am spirit soul” will remain eternally beyond our comprehension if we pursue such realizations with our blunt material senses and without the mercy of Kṛṣṇa and our spiritual master.

*ataḥ śrī-kṛṣṇa-nāmādi*  
*na bhaved grāhyam indriyaiḥ*  
*sevonmukhe hi jihvādau*  
*svayam eva sphuraty adaḥ*

**The Lord is not captured by the gross materialist or mental speculator. He reveals Himself only to the surrendered soul whose life and senses have become purified by the process of devotional service.**

Had you not voluntarily chosen to accept the hardships imposed upon you by attempting the almost impossible in the Western world, not one of your disciples would have had even the slightest glimpse of genuine spiritual existence. What to speak of knowing how to purify our senses, none of us even recognized the need.

*Ahaṁ sarvasya prabhavaḥ.* Kṛṣṇa says, “I am the source of both the spiritual and material worlds. Everything emanates from Me.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us Kṛṣṇa in so many forms. Have you not then certainly given us everything? You have given us the holy name, with all the potencies of the Supreme Lord contained within its vibration, regardless of our ability to perceive the fact or not. You have given us the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and with it, access to the literary incarnation of the Lord.

Despite our heritage of perversity and impersonalism, you have carefully given us even from the beginning the Tenth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in the form of *Kṛṣṇa* book. Because of your purity, you were able to present these intimate pastimes of the Lord in such a way that we never made the mistake of thinking Lord Kṛṣṇa to be ordinary or mundane, and you thus elevated us in blissful Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You have given us the direct association of the Lord in the transcendental forms of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, Gaura-Nitāi, and Lord Jagannātha, Baladeva and Subhadrā. Without your gift of Deity worship, the *arcā-vigraha* forms of the Lord would be conspicuous in this Western world only by their absence. Now, by your mercy, people’s eyes everywhere are becoming cleansed by Their Lordships’ *darśana.*

You have given us the means for maintaining body and soul, teaching us “not to eat but to honor” the sumptuous *prasādam* of‚ Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and calling for the help of‚ Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga.

Seeing our hearts and consciousness heavy, carrying the burden of lifetimes of sin, you gave us the wonderful worship of Śrīmatī Tulasī-devī, whose circumambulation can purify us of even the greatest of sins—killing a *brāhmaṇa.*

All of these gifts are actually nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. In giving us the means, you have given us the end. There can be no doubt, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us everything—and much more than we deserve.

Even our life’s activities, now based upon maintaining and expanding the movement you have begun, would cease to exist were it not for your lifelong example of preaching despite all impediments. Even up to your last days on this planet, as you lay on your bed, you translated *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* into English and constantly discussed the printing and distribution of your books.

Actually, to attempt to list your contributions to our life is impossible, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your instructions, example, and desires make up every single facet of our lives. We will know we are actually advancing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness when we begin to actually perceive how dependent and indebted the entire world is to you.

Your ability to hear purely and without motive or envy and to speak without change has obliged the whole world to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and you yourself confirmed this in an interview in the San Francisco airport. One reporter asked you how many disciples you had, and you replied that actually everyone was your disciple. Some were aware of it, and some were not.

Therefore we desire to glorify you unlimitedly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day celebrating your appearance, and we beg you to chasten our hearts and minds and bless us with undeviating adherence to your instructions and example for the duration of our life and thereafter.

*Your eternal servants, the devotees in Portugal*

Praghoṣa dāsa / Anantadeva dāsa / Prema-prayojana dāsa / Bhakta Naresh / Rādhāṅga-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Isabelle / Donna Maria de Lourdes Silva

## Puebla

Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You have come as the best of the ambassadors from the spiritual world, the brightest amongst the luminaries (as the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam),* to dissipate the darkness caused by the mode of ignorance. You are the person Bhāgavata, the personification of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* You are the most beloved and faithful servant of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and you have come as the greatest ocean of mercy!

As the most benevolent father who shelters millions of souls at his home, ISKCON!  
The most lucid and penetrating critic in the midst of a civilization of *yavanas* and *mlecchas.*  
The best among *sādhus!*  
The best among philosophers!  
The most aristocratic among the swans!  
The one most aware of the Kali-yuga madness!  
The most perfect friend of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, with whom you wander through Vraja and whom you serve with loving feelings!  
The worshipable master among the intellects!  
The Vaiṣṇava awaited by so many other great *ācāryas* such as Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, whose desires you fulfilled!  
The greatest recipient of Śrī Caitanya’s mercy!  
The best ambassador of *bhakti-yoga* (as wisely described by the journalist of the *Butler Eagle* upon your arrival in America)!  
The giver of the matchless gift, Kṛṣṇa consciousness!

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, beloved spiritual master, on this glorious day we remember that you are the captain of the ship, capable of taking us on a one-way trip over this ocean of nescience to the supreme destiny, the supreme abode, Goloka Vṛndāvana!

Millions of tongues will not be sufficient to chant your glories, and millions of ears will not suffice to hear all your glories. Nevertheless, permit us to humbly chant for all eternity:

Jaya Prabhupāda, Jaya Prabhupāda, Jaya Prabhupāda, Jaya Prabhupāda, Jaya Prabhupāda!

Your humble servants of ISKCON Puebla Mandira

## Rhode Island

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracārine*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Taking birth as a conditioned soul in this material

world means being born into the darkness of ignorance. What to speak of material birth, we have been born in the West, in the age of Kali. In the *śāstras* it is stated, *kalau śūdra-sambhavaḥ:* in Kali-yuga everyone is born a *śūdra.* We, however, would not even be considered civilized human beings by the Vedic standard, but rather *dvi-pāda-paśus:* two-legged beasts.

Being trained from infancy as gross materialists, we became conditioned to accept the body as the self and the gratification of the senses as the goal of life. Absorbed in the animal propensities of eating, sleeping, mating, and defending, we were controlled by the urges of the mind and senses. Thus forced to engage in all varieties of sinful activity, we were paving our way to hell.

Ignorant and uneducated in the matter of transcendental knowledge, we accepted the subtle material platform to be spiritual, and were consequently deceived by unscrupulous cheaters in the guise of so many teachers, religionists, *gurus,* and *yogīs.* Thus, even in the pursuit of knowledge we found ourselves in a hopeless condition, blinded by the darkness of ignorance.

By the causeless mercy of the Supreme Lord and Your Divine Grace, our eyes have now been forced open by the torchlight of Vedic knowledge. The *Vedas* are called *nigama-kalpa-taru,* or the desire tree of Vedic literatures, because they contain all things knowable to man. They supply all the necessary information for a peaceful and prosperous life directed toward a scientific understanding of the highest knowledge of man’s spiritual nature.

The Vedic knowledge, however, has to be understood by hearing it from a bona fide spiritual master in the line of disciplic succession. This is enjoined in the *Upaniṣads:* one must approach a spiritual master to learn the transcendental science. No amount of scholarship or mental speculation can afford one access to the knowledge of the *Vedas.* This information about the Supreme Absolute Truth is beyond the purview of our blunt material senses and subtle minds, and it is, therefore, only available to those to whom it is revealed. In the *Purāṇas* it is stated that the Supreme Personality of Godhead reveals Himself to those who have pleased Him by transcendental loving service. But in our materially contaminated state it is not possible to directly serve the Lord, what to speak of pleasing Him.

The mercy of Kṛṣṇa is coming down through the mercy of the spiritual master, and by pleasing the *guru,* who is the pure representative of God,one gains the favor of Kṛṣṇa. So by your unlimited compassion and sincere desire to execute the order of your spiritual master, you traveled to the West, and then all around the world, spreading the light of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, as taught by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, is the essence of Vedic knowledge and the only hope for the misguided souls in this age of Kali.

Without the mercy of Your Divine Grace we would still be counted amongst the numerous living entities who are floundering in illusion, suffering the pangs of material existence. As long as we follow your instructions, we remain in the Kṛṣṇa sun and are protected from *māyā.* If we deviate, however, then we will again come under the grip of material nature and her stringent laws. Therefore, we are taking this auspicious day as an opportunity to pray for your continued mercy, so that we may never forget your divine teachings.

*Your disciples and granddisciples,*

Agrāṇī Swami / Keśihantā dāsa / Vālmīki dāsa / Dīna-śaraṇa dāsa / Jagadānanda Paṇḍita dāsa / Vidyābhūṣaṇa dāsa / Vidura dāsa dāsa / Moses Nārāyaṇa / Bhakta Jonathon

**Congregation**

Ron, Donna and Kim Menard / Chris Beetle / Dr. Rādhā Krishna / Dr. and Mrs. Bhattacharjee

## Riverina

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Your lotus feet are the only shelter for the conditioned souls. As we see the Kali-yuga increasing every day, what hope is there for the living entity’s liberation from this bondage? Everywhere one looks there is simply *māyā*—the agents of Kali, publicizing meat-eating, intoxication, illicit sex and gambling to degrade the population more and more.

The only hope is Your Divine Grace. With the torchlight of transcendental knowledge you pave the way through the darkness to a life of purity. “It’s very simple,” you say. “Chant, dance, feast and work for Krṣna’s satisfaction, and your life will be sublime.” Never in history has there been such a personality so pure, with a message so simple, to solve the problems inflicting the world. Many so-called great men tried to solve the problems with material arrangements, such as economics, philosophy, etc., but all failed due to their missing the soul’s connection to Kṛṣṇa. Only you, Prabhupāda, have given the proper solution. You have the insight and knowledge, the love of Kṛṣṇa to impart, by which the living entity can give up this search for happiness in the material world.

So as the Kali-yuga increases, we must take shelter of your instructions more and more, to try to counteract *māyā* and preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This is your desire, and the only way to please you is to preach, and thus return oneself and everyone else back to Godhead.

*Wishing to remain your eternal servants,*  
The devotees of ISKCON New Gauḍadeśa Farm,

## St. Louis

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracārine*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious of auspicious days for all your ISKCON devotees.

We were thinking that since your departure from this place we are feeling your separation greatly. The excitement of your personal association is missing, and therefore we feel morose. That moroseness is dissipated, however, when we hear your instructions, engage in your service, and especially when we remember your līlā.

I remember in 1976 at the Vṛndāvana festival you were in your room and one brahmacārī asked me if I could guard on the outside porch while he took *prasādam.* I jumped at the opportunity for some personal service, yet didn’t know what the job entailed, since I had never guarded your quarters at that time in Vṛndāvana. I was sitting, reading Seventh Canto, thinking how fortunate I was, when all of a sudden I heard a bell ringing. Not knowing what it was, I just kept reading as the bell kept ringing. Then, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to the screen door and I rose, “Jaya, Śrīla Prabhupāda.” You looked very grave, and I didn’t know what I had done wrong, so you said, “You are sitting there, and I am sitting here, and I am ringing, and I must come to you?” I was shocked as you repeated, “I am the spiritual master and you are the disciple, and I am ringing, and I must come to you?” I was horrified. I said, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, I had no idea. One brahmacārī.. .” You cut me off and gravely said, “I am an old man, and you are a young man, and there are six of you, and I am ringing and I must come to you?” I was so sorry I just begged, “Please forgive me, I didn’t know. ..” You sharply said, “Go find Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa!” All I could see was the gold tooth in your transcendentally angered lotus mouth. You returned to your room and I felt bewildered, realizing I had committed a grave offense. Then I thought that as all the other servants must be at *prasādam,* I couldn’t leave the porch to get Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa, or no one would be guarding. One boy came by, and I called him over and said, “Prabhu, you gotta go get Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa at the Fogel Ashram, because Prabhupāda wants him and...” He answered, “No habla Inglese, prabhu.” I was fried, because *prasādam* was at the Fogel Ashram and I couldn’t leave to go get the devotee there, and no other devotees were around. Then the bell rang again, and I ran into the room. As I attempted to offer obeisances, you said, “Where is Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa?” I was bewildered and didn’t know whether to answer the question or offer obeisances first, since I didn’t want to commit another offense. I answered, “ He’s taking *prasādam* and I couldn’t find him and . . .” “Go to the kitchen and get someone,” you said. I turned to go, and there were two doorways, so I choose the right one, which led to the servant’s quarters, and you chastised, “No, the other door!” I felt like a fool and remembered the instruction that one should feel like a fool in front of the spiritual master but not act like a fool. I had also acted like a fool and considered that I had thrown away a great opportunity to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. So I ran in the back and luckily found Hari Śauri Prabhu preparing something, and I urged him, “You’ve got to come right away. Prabhupāda needs you immediately.” He sent me around, and I went back to guarding the door on the porch. As I sat there I tried to read, but my mind was reeling, and my eyes just stared at the book as I thought that I had committed the greatest offense to a pure devotee in the *dhāma.* I thought that I’d probably be thrown out of the movement; Prabhupāda is displeased with me. Then I suddenly felt like crying, but I thought if I did something very good, I could make up for my bungle. You were to take *prasādam* at one man’s place next door and would be coming out in ten minutes, the servant informed me. There was one big umbrella that the servant said should be held to shade you from the sun. I made up my mind to hold that umbrella for you, and in that way be forgiven. As you were getting ready to walk out of your Vṛndāvana quarters, one *sannyāsī* came and told me to give him the umbrella to hold for you. I reluctantly submitted and felt completely dejected. As you came out of the room I offered *daṇḍavats* and began repeatedly praying in anxiety, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, please forgive me. Don’t kick me away. Please let me serve you more.” As I rose up, you were just passing by, and you softly said, “Jaya.” I knew then that you knew my heart and would give me another chance to please you. You are so kind that you allow a fool like myself to serve you. So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are all begging you to allow us to serve and please you more and more. Don’t kick us away, please. Destroy our foolishness and let us perform some activity that will make you proud of your disciples.

*Your eternal servants at New Kṛṣṇaloka,*

## Salvador

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracārine*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

On this auspicious day of your divine appearance we would like to thank you very much for being able to participate in this big movement for Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Now, since you have established in this material world the mission to please the desire of Lord Caitanya, each and every sincere soul has got the big chance to make his life perfect and attain eternal happiness. Although insignificant, we feel very much proud of the exalted grandfather we have, who has given humanity the biggest gift.

Of everything you have given us, no doubt the service unto your beloved son Hṛdayānanda Goswami, our beloved spiritual master, is the cause of all our good fortune. By his mercy we have been able to appreciate a spark of your unlimited transcendental qualities. As a perfect disciple from a perfect spiritual master, Hṛdayānanda Goswami could understand the inner spirit of your mission, and now he is teaching us how to please you, the founder-*ācārya,* by always performing *saṅkīrtana* as the center of all our activities and desires. He once said, “I want ISKCON Brazil to merge in this divine consciousness of love for Śrīla Prabhupāda by doing *saṅkīrtana. I* would like everyone to think, feel, and desire this book distribution. Never let *saṅkīrtana* be out of the center of your mind, and then you will always live with Śrīla Prabhupāda in Kṛṣṇa’s heart.” *Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo / yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi.* Without the spiritual master being pleased, our life remains futile and morose.

On this auspicious commemoration of your divine appearance, we get together to strengthen our remembrance of you and beg for a drop of your unlimited mercy, and then we can become more sincere in our aim to serve this great cause, Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

*Your most fallen grandchildren,*

Advaita dāsa brahmacārī / Hari-cakra dāsa brahmacārī / Govinda-gāna dāsa brahmacārī / Rāma-preṣṭha dāsa brahmacārī / Bhakta Dhīra / Bhakta Jorge / Bhakta Antonio / Gaṅgā-devī dāsī / Ānandārṇava-devī dāsī / Śrī Kṛṣṇa-prasāda-devī dāsī

## San Diego

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracārine*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

It has been said that when a great *ācārya* appears he brings order to chaos and upon his departure that order again falls back into chaos. There are those who would say you have left and chaos is pending, threatening to envelop us again. But that is not our perception here. Once you were asked, “What do you pray when you go before the Deities, Śrīla Prabhupāda?” and you answered out of your sublime humility (and to teach us), “I am praying to Rādhārāṇī that I will never fall down.” In the same mood we are fervently praying that we will never forget you, because to forget you, or minimize your instructions, *means* to inevitably fall down.

And how to remember you? By serving your mission. You told Bhagavān Goswami, “I have given you the framework; all you have to do is decorate it. And I have given you the decorations; all you have to do is hang them.” Here in New Govardhana we are trying to do just that—by simply distributing your books, holding festivals, and distributing sumptuous *prasādam.* We are feeling your mercy and presence more and more each day.

As we grow older we can feel personally and observe collectively the ravages of the “indefatigable material energy” as it crushes the illusory hopes and plans of the conditioned souls. Your words are the only shelter in this place, which is “temporary and full of miseries.” As fine music or art can only be truly appreciated with an educated eye or ear, in the same way, as we are becoming purified by service and qualified by detachment, we are experiencing the relief, the shelter, and the satisfaction of being situated in our *nitya-svarūpa kṛṣṇa-dāsa* position. You have given us everything, and all you ask in return is that we take it wholeheartedly and distribute it to others. Your mercy is all we are made of.

*Your servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhāri at New Govardhana*

## San Francisco

**Prabhupāda’s Lotus Feet**

The earth is in a tumble, Its churches falling down.  
The answers are all mumbled, The truth is underground;  
The legions of the liars. As far as I can see,  
The only place that’s safe Is Prabhupāda’s lotus feet!  
The home is torn and tattered, The babe is killed unborn.  
The wife is beat and battered, The husband’s pride is gone.  
Their children snarl like animals And gnash their sharpened teeth.  
The only place that’s safe Is Prabhupāda’s lotus feet!  
The cow is slaughtered mercilessly; Her blood runs in the street.  
And men have stricken from their hearts That “blessed are the meek.”  
The rich, they dine with jewels adorned, Their dogs beg them for meat.  
The only place that’s safe Is Prabhupāda’s lotus feet!  
The false and bogus teachers Have led the mass astray.  
They tell them “You can have it all And never have to pay.”  
They lead your children onward With words and smiles so sweet.  
The only place that’s safe Is Prabhupāda’s lotus feet!  
The sky is full of thunder; Harsh words and bullets fly.  
Your food is full of poison; Great horrors fill the eye.  
There is no shelter for you fools; The cycle is complete.  
The only place that’s safe Is Prabhupāda’s lotus feet!

*Your servants in ISKCON San Francisco*

## Sao Paulo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your holy lotus feet. You are the bona fide representative of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Only by your sincerity and pure love for the Supreme Lord and your spiritual master could you teach us how to become perfect. You crossed the ocean and came to a hopeless civilization forgetful of its original nature. When we hear about the transcendental activities of your life before coming to this Western world, we are filled up with enthusiasm, fired up to preach. It was a lifetime of preparation just to give us, the fallen conditioned souls, the seed of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, had once said that if one of his disciples became Kṛṣṇa conscious, his mission on earth would be successful. And, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you really made your spiritual master successful. You really pleased him. By your purity you could understand his heart. Actually, you are purity personified in this world of cheaters.

We may took forever and we will never find any substitute for you in anything you have done. And you have done everything. Looking backwards now we can see how much strength and hardship you must have gone through just to form this ISKCON society. You showed us by your personal example how to give up everything useless for Kṛṣṇa’s service and accept anything which could be useful even though it may be so painful.

As the founder-*ācārya* for this big Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, you had to do things never done before. Actually, not only did you have to fight in these materialistic Western countries, but you also had to flght against your own Godbrothers. Sadly to say, they had forgotten the real mission of your Guru Mahārāja. But we are very much proud to have you as our eternal master. No doubt you were that big personality predicted decades ago by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura to flood the world with this mass *saṅkīrtana.*

On this auspicious day of your divine appearance among ourselves, we beg for your mercy so that we can go on with this *bṛhad-mṛdaṅga* you have established in ISKCON. You always said that the publication and the distribution of this transcendental literature should be our main concern, and we are very fortunate to have as our guide and master here in Brazil your beloved disciple Hṛdayānanda Goswami, who never forgets this as our first priority. Just by hearing your pastimes in publishing books—you had to endeavor so hard just to start your *Back to Godhead* magazine and your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and then distributing them—you worked so hard to bring the pure message to the kingdom of the *yavanas.* O *Ś*rīla Prabhupāda, you are as glorious as the Supreme Personality of Godhead! Please do not ever let us go astray. Do not let us return to this place of *māyā,* but let us remain always serving you and the servants of Your Divine Grace birth after birth, under the shelter of your lotus feet.

*Your servants from Sao Paulo, Brazil,*

## Scandinavia

Dear Srīla Prabhupāda,

Please allow me to purify myself by offering sincere words of glorification on the auspicious occasion of your eighty-seventh appearance day. Since my offering is amidst those of your pure disciples, I am praying that you will find some merit in it.

It is explained in the *śāstra* that there are different classes of devotees. One is the materialistic neophyte who worships the Supreme Lord in the temple but does not realize that all living entities are His parts and parcels. He is motivated for material benediction or liberation from the suffering of this *saṁsāra* cycle.

Another class is that of the greatly advanced self-realized soul who loves Kṛṣṇa knowing full well that the perfection of life is to be always engaged in the service of the Lord irrevocably. Such a devotee is, however, so devoid of false ego that he sees that everyone is serving Kṛṣṇa but him.

Between these two types is the preacher who sees Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Personality of Godhead and worships Him. He sees also the envious class of demons who oppose devotional service and avoids them. He sees also the devotees and makes friends and serves them; and fourthly, he sees the innocent and preaches to them that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the topmost engagement for all and factually the only alternative in this material world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is undoubtedly belonging to the first class, the perfected class, but this I do not find so significant, or more correctly said, is so far beyond my comprehension that this aspect of our spiritual master is practically inconceivable to me from the present position. However, Prabhupāda kindly assumed the second-class position of the preacher, and that I find very enlivening and very real and comprehensible, by his grace. How he challenged everyone! The impersonalist, the *smārta,* the Christian, the communist, and even the scientist, and defeated them all in their own field. Just as Nimāi Paṇḍita admitted humbly that He was an “ordinary student of grammar” before Keśava Kaśmīrī, because He was *śruti-dhara* He knew from authoritative sources how to criticize the literary scholar. So Prabhupāda taught by his own examples the superiority of obtaining knowledge by the descending process *(avaroha)* as opposed to empiricism *(pratyakṣa).* Yet he was no dogmatist. He was willing to put aside the scriptures and argue on the basis of reason and logic.

On this auspicious occasion I beg you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to please grant me a place amongst your disciples on Lord Caitanya’s *saṇkīrtana* party. This is the Prabhupāda that is enthusing my heart so weak from material attachments. But when in the spirit of preaching, by his grace I come upon an innocent soul who responds to the timeless message of the *paramparā,* then my faith is restored in his instruction that there is factually an innocent class who will accept Kṛṣṇa without difficulty if presented strictly in accordance with the principles. I pray that Prabhupāda will grant all of us eternal engagement in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* party.

I am praying to Prabhupāda that his books fully manifest the Sudarśana *cakra* and destroy the demoniac mentality pervading this planet at present. Let the demons be destroyed by the power of the holy name, so that all the devotees can everywhere celebrate the victory of *śrī kṛṣṇa saṅkīrtana* on the occasion of our spiritual master’s divine appearance. I offer my repeated obeisances at the lotus feet of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

## Seattle

*akṣṇoḥ phalaṁ tvādrśa-darśanaṁ hi*  
*tanoḥ phalaṁ tvādrśa-gātra-saṅgaḥ*  
*jihvā-phalaṁ tvādrśa-kīrtanaṁ hi*  
*su-durlabhā bhāgavatā hi loke*

**“My dear Vaiṣṇava, seeing a person like you is the perfection of one’s eyesight. Touching your lotus feet is the perfection of the sense of touch. Glorifying your good qualities is the tongue’s real activity, for in the material world it is very difficult to find a pure devotee of the Lord.”**

If by seeing, by touching, or by glorifying the qualities of a pure devotee of the Lord one can attain the complete perfection of his existence, then what can be said of the attainment of a person who has surrendered his entire life and soul at the lotus feet of such a great devotee to engage in his eternal service? It is that great fortune of eternal servitude to the pure devotee of the Lord for which today we are trying to humbly express our gratitude.

The human form of body is very rarely achieved, and within human society someone who is actually interested in liberation from material bondage is very rarely seen. Still more rare than he is a devotee of the Lord, and out of hundreds and thousands of such Vaiṣṇavas, there may be one who is cent-percent engaged in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Such a personality is His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, to whom we are offering our respectful homage.

Human society is divided into four *varṇas* and four *āśramas,* out of which the *brāhmaṇas* are considered the best, because they represent the Supreme Lord. A *brāhmaṇa* who knows the *Vedas* is superior, but better still is one who knows the purport of the *Vedas.* A *brāhmaṇa* who can dissipate all doubts is still superior, but still better is one who fully engages in the prescribed duties of a *brāhmaṇa.* Better than him is one who is free from all material contamination, but best of all is a *brāhmaṇa* who engages in the pure devotional service of Kṛṣṇa without expectation of any reward. Such a great soul is His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, to whom we offer our respectful homage.

Knowledge of Kṛṣṇa is not available to those who are filled with desires for material enjoyment and liberation. Such knowledge is withheld from their understanding, and therefore they think of the Supreme as impersonal Brahman, as diverse in many demigods who can award benedictions for material enjoyment, or as the universal form. However, one who considers the various potencies of the Lord as Supreme without reasoning that any energy must have a potent source, and who therefore never tries to investigate that supreme source, is an impersonalist and is considered less intelligent. All energies have their potent source, and the fact that the supreme source is personal is proven by the fact that personality exists everywhere within His creation. If within God’s creation we find personality, then the Supreme Person is the Lord Himself, who is the cause of all causes. Knowledge of that Supreme Person is not revealed to the mystic *yogīs,* or the mental speculators, or those who thoroughly study the *Vedas,* or those who perform severe austerities and penances, or those who give charity or accept the *sannyāsa* order. Such knowledge is revealed only to one who is a fully surrendered soul at the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Such a great devotee is His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, to whom we offer our respectful homage.

A conditioned soul wandering throughout this universe becomes extremely fortunate when Kṛṣṇa favors him by appearing externally as the *guru* while continuing to instruct him internally as the Supersoul. The *guru,* therefore, is directly the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead, who is so qualified in that he repeats the instructions of Kṛṣṇa without change and in that he perfectly follows such instructions in his personal life. Actually, the *guru* has no private life, because while alone he is immersed in the glorification of Kṛṣṇa, and while amidst the general public his worship continues by the same glorification, and therefore there is no dichotomy in his behavior. He is simply addicted to Kṛṣṇa, and nothing else. Such a spiritual master can easily deliver Kṛṣṇa and love of Kṛṣṇa to the disciple. We offer our respectful homage unto the lotus feet of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is the *guru* of the entire universe.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, through millions of tongues we cannot properly express your glories or our gratitude for saving us from an abominable, hellish life. You have given us the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the worship of His eternal forms as Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Nīla-Mādhava, and the great opportunity to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this material world. Here in Seattle Yātrā it is a daily struggle to maintain the top standard of Deity worship and preaching effort which you have prescribed for us, due to our shortage of manpower, but that struggle is our life’s blood and solemn ecstasy, knowing that it is but a token representation of service at Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet. Let that struggle to maintain and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Seattle as well as life itself be our respectful offering of homage at your lotus feet on this Vyāsa-pūjā day, although we are unable to properly do it.

Your servants at Seattle Ydtrā,

Śukadeva dāsa / Patraka dāsa / Kaṁsāri dāsa / Dāmaghoṣa dāsa / Anantātmā dāsa / Deva-darśana dāsa / Bhakta Mike / Kāntimatī-devī dāsī / Śrīdāmā / Jīva / Bhakti / Gaura-pūrṇimā

## State College

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our respectful obeisances to you, the most confidential servitor of Lord Kṛṣṇa and our compassionate savior. Who else came to these dark and demoniac lands? Who else took the great risk and infinite pains to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the millions who were drowning in the shoreless ocean—who were certainly not qualified by their *karma* to receive this most matchless gift, but who have inconceivably received it nonetheless, due only to your causeless mercy?

We cling fast to your ISKCON movement, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for we know that its life is the only hope for the world and for ourselves. And we know that its life is your life, and that you are the source of its life, for you are the eternal root of this beautiful, flowering tree. Those separated limbs are only sad before our eyes, like the cut flowers in the florist’s shop awaiting the inevitable withering and death. How will they ever produce the fruit to feed the millions?

O master, remotely we are the servants of Your Divine Grace, like some migrant laborers or external groundkeepers, giving our time and labor but still alienated somehow, not of the family or intimate circle of friends. It is only by your causeless mercy that we have this valuable opportunity, however, this auspicious chance to serve in your employ. And we have gained an understanding that the blessed circle of intimate association and loving service has nothing to do with the physical proximity or external nature of the service but that it is open to anyone who sincerely wants it.

In spite of this, our transient minds, with their long-standing habits of roving and failing to ultimately surrender, have left us dawdling at the perimeters of your estate, mechanically doing little odd jobs without the fullness of enthusiasm and the joy of fulfillment that comes from the successful creation of your pleasure. That creation is real art, but we are mere laborers, separated from that ecstatic moment of creation by our ever-present reflections on how each of our acts may create our own pleasures or pains.

Removed from spontaneous loving service to you by our own selfish desires—for which we must each accept full personal responsibility—we are unable to relish its nectar, due to the bilious envy which rises to cover our senses when we contemplate the activities of your really dear servants, whose humble and contrite hearts pay a far richer tribute of devotion than ours ever have, a gift infinitely more acceptable than our elaborate adorations.

Our one hope, then, is that you will continue to pry open our darkened eyes, allowing us to glimpse your smiling compassion, thus filling us with eagerness to be attached always to your loving service. Our pretentious plans are the seeds of our continued anxiety and uncertainty. Please

purify us so that devotion and love for you may manifest in us like the rising sun, dispelling this cold, gloomy cloud of *ahaṅkāra* in which we wander in separation.

Please instill in us the infinite patience and tolerance for dealing with others that you have exhibited towards us. Just like a kindly parent teaching his child, you urge us, “Come on, you can do it. That’s it ... Oh ... but that’s all right, come on, get back up, stand up. Very good! Now, take your hands and do like this. Now, can you say Hare Kṛṣṇa? Yes, sure you can ... just like this: Hare Kṛṣṇa. Now you try it ... yes, very nice. Very nice.”

Please, dear master, let us understand the humility and forgiveness which are the matrix upon which all our spiritual dealings will take place. Let us learn to be Vaiṣṇava *brāhmaṇas,* in whose persons the cycle of intolerance and revenge and envy finds its end, being willfully absorbed in humble service. Kick us as often as necessary to realize these things, the sooner the better, but please don’t kick us away.

*Your servants in State College,*

Stambha dāsa brahmacārī / Uddhava dāsa brahmacārī / Pūrṇānanda dāsa brahmacārī / Bhakta Michael / Bhakta Steve K. / Bhaktin Sandhya / Bhaktin Lisa

Give us strength and determination, because we ourselves haven’t any qualifications. Remembrance of you is all that we need to develop the strength to succeed in our preaching programs. Please guide us so that we can serve you forever.

We see the world awakening to spiritual consciousness by the guidance of your empowered representatives. The doubts of innocent people are being driven away by the effulgent explanations of your surrendered disciples.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, always appear in our restless minds, and remind us to clutch your lotus feet, for they are the perfect shelter. *hā hā prabhu koro doyā, deho more pada-chāyā.*
