## Switzerland

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Our beloved spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

On this most auspicious event of your appearance day, we throw ourselves in the dust of your lotus feet, full of love and enthusiasm to glorify Your Divine Grace.

It is a fact that the spiritual master saves his disciple. He actually saves him. It is compulsory for the disciple to always remember why he came to this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Why did he come? Because his spiritual master saved him. It is the personal love, the endeavor, the struggle and austerity of the spiritual master that saves his disciple. How can the disciple ever forget this mercy? Here, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we suggest to accept the word *impossible* in your Vaiṣṇava dictionary. It is *impossible* for a sincere disciple to ever forget the mercy of his spiritual master. Because the spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa, it is Kṛṣṇa Himself who saves the living entity.

*jive sākṣāt nāhi tāte guru caittya-rūpe*  
*śikṣā-guru haya kṛṣṇa-mahānte svarūpe*

**“Since one cannot visually experience the presence of the Supersoul, He appears before us as a liberated devotee. Such a spiritual master is no other than Kṛṣṇa Himself.”**

As soon as the disciple neglects or does not appreciate the mercy of the spiritual master, he again turns away from Kṛṣṇa. He can never find any other shelter.

What is the difficulty in accepting and glorifying Your Divine Grace as the most exalted Vaiṣṇava amongst all other Vaiṣṇavas, as the most elevated pure devotee of the highest order? How much more evidence do we need? At such an advanced age you came to the West. So many difficulties you underwent. Still you did not give up. Instead, you opened more than a hundred centers all over the world, you changed thousands of *mlecchas* into Vaiṣṇavas, you traveled many times around the world to preach, establish and organize this movement. You initiated so many projects, and now gradually they become manifest and are able to engage unlimited more devotees all over the world. And above all, you worked so hard and tirelessly to translate and print more than seventy volumes of Vaiṣṇava literature. All the perfection of Vaiṣṇava philosophy is contained in these books. Everything is in your books. Once, one of your Godbrothers was being asked about his highest realization of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He answered that his highest realization was of the topics discussed between Rāmānanda Rāya and Caitanya Mahāprabhu. This conversation was printed even twice by your own Book Trust. Your books are so potent. Just by reading them, people all over the world feel the urge to surrender to Kṛṣṇa. That is the actual proof of the spiritual value of your transcendental books. These books have already created a spiritual revolution in society. Just as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in His childhood splashed water at the *yogīs* who were sitting in so-called meditation at the shore of mother Ganges, you are also splashing dull and inert people all over the world with the ecstatic waves of your transcendental books, so that they may wake up from their material and spiritual slumber to engage their senses in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Of course, your books do not contain topics that are contaminated by perverted spiritual feelings or any other deviations. That is your mercy. Those who do not desire pure, unalloyed devotional service may be illusioned to think that there is something missing within your books. All your commentaries are pure and spotless. Still, your books supply sufficient knowledge to protect us against any type of contamination. Your books, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the eternal guide and protection for the devotees. These books are your heart and soul, because they are the embodiment of your own perfect surrender to the instructions of your spiritual master. It was the personal request of your Guru Mahārāja to go to the West and print books. Therefore you always pushed book production and distribution, and still you are pushing. Who can even begin to estimate the deep love you have for your spiritual master? These books, temples, devotees, etc., are all fruits of your love for *guru* and Kṛṣṇa. If one simply comes in contact with or just even appreciates these fruits, one’s dormant love for Kṛṣṇa is awakened. They are so dear to the Lord, and He always protects them carefully. Whoever tries to do some harm to these transcendental fruits of love of God is immediately doomed. He will be punished by the Lord, as we shall understand from a very instructive analogy.

There is a beautiful, clear lake and a nice, colorful lotus flower. The sun is shining down on that lotus flower, and by the nourishment of the sunshine and the water of the lake, the lotus flower comes into full bloom. Now, if somehow the lake dries out, if there is no more water, the same sun destroys the beautiful lotus flower very quickly. That is the situation of a disciple who rejects the mercy of his spiritual master. The mercy of the spiritual master is compared to the lake, the disciple to the lotus flower, and Kṛṣṇa to the sun. The conditioned soul can never at any time approach Kṛṣṇa directly—only through the mercy of the spiritual master. Some people may say that this is a very limited and narrow-minded process, but the truth is that it is Kṛṣṇa’s kindness, because He made it so easy for us to surrender to Him. And only by total surrender to Kṛṣṇa, to Śrīla Prabhupāda, is there no room for any speculation, misinterpretation, doubt or misunderstanding.

There is only one type of literature—all the books printed by the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust; one *mantra*—the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra;* one movement—ISKCON; and one spiritual master—he who totally surrenders to your lotus feet and carries them on his head eternally. And you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are eternally glorified as the founder-*ācārya* of this movement, the spiritual master of all other spiritual masters, and as the most elevated *paramahaṁsa* of the highest order. This is the actual truth, and those who do not agree are simply closing their eyes, full of enviousness. They don’t want to see what is clearly evident, as mentioned in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta:*

*dekhiyā nā dekhe yata abhaktera gaṇa*  
*ulūke nā dekhe yena sūryera kiraṇa*

**“But faithless unbelievers do not see what is clearly evident, just as owls do not see the rays of the sun.”**

Therefore, we pray at your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to always keep our eyes open with the torchlight of transcendental truth, so that we do not deviate even an inch from our determination to carry out your orders, whatever may happen.

## Sydney

Dear Srila Prabhupāda,

Abhay Charan De you were named (one who is fearless, having taken shelter at Viṣṇu’s lotus feet), not by chance or out of some passing sentiment, but to declare to the world that a fearless preacher who would soon challenge the supremacy of Māyā-devī’s domain had now taken his birth.

To deliver all the wretched conditioned souls rotting here in the material world—this you declared as your mission in life. And taking up the banner of Śrī Caitanya, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as His invincible general, created an army of dedicated followers and declared war on *māyā’s* kingdom.

“Armed with the weapon of knowledge, O Arjuna, stand and fight.” In the *Gītā*, Kṛṣṇa had spoken. So these books are our bombs, you have explained. “Just as in wartime the bombs are raining from the sky, so in the same way, we must rain these books down on the heads of the conditioned souls.”

In New York you told that man on the park bench that you had temples, you had books—they were merely separated by time. So already, by your mercy the whole universe is delivered. It is now simply a question of time, and that is all.

Who can fathom the depths of your compassion, Śrīla Prabhupāda? Like the sun, your mercy shines everywhere. Who is there that can escape the casting of your divine net? And of us, you have molded fishermen of men. By your life’s example you have shown that you were the very personification of bold and fearless preaching, and you have instilled this spirit in those that are “your men.”

You wrote, “If you feel at all indebted to me, then you should preach vigorously like me. That is the proper way to repay me.”

We are running to catch up to your lotus feet and are simply praying, “Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us not lose sight of your glorious preaching mission.”

*Your eternal servants at ISKCON Sydney,*

## Tehran

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our repeated humble obeisances at your lotus feet. As the years are going by, we are growing spiritually and have the opportunity to hear your unlimited glories. Every year on this eventful day we realize more that we have been saved from the cruel hands *of māyā* by you. Here in this *yātrā* none of us had the opportunity to associate with you personally. But we are hearing your glories from your books and your disciples. The more your disciples take shelter at your lotus feet, the more your glorious activities become manifested to us. Here in this country we are facing so many difficulties. There is shortage of food, and they do not let us preach, etc. And as we go along, we appreciate the hardship Your Divine Grace went through when first you went to the West. As Kuntīdevī prays to Lord Kṛṣṇa for difficulties so she can appreciate and surrender to Kṛṣṇa, so we are praying to Your Divine Grace to bestow upon us your mercy, that we may take shelter *of* your lotus feet and serve you in a cooperative mood. Here, every day we are encouraged by your personal teachings in how we should act and not be fearful *of* anything. You are teaching us how we should be dependent on Kṛṣṇa. You showed all *of* these things personally. Although with your mercy we are engaged in serving you, still your unlimited glories remain covered to us. Please be kind to us and allow us to remain in the ocean *of* your transcendental mercy.

*We beg to remain your eternal servants,*

## Toronto

A humble offering to our eternal spiritual master, His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, on the blessed day of the anniversary of his appearance.

Caught in the maze of material creation,  
Helplessly searching to find a way out;  
Finally tired of all degradation,  
Some few jīvas asked what life was about.  
We hadn’t a clue as to whom we could turn  
To get some relief from this self-imposed prison;  
For real honest truth our hearts all did yearn.  
To effect our release, a great saint had arisen.  
He conquered all obstacles put in his way,  
So strong was his wish to serve the Supreme Lord,  
And to aid in the coming dawn of the day  
When in every town, Kṛṣṇa’s name would be heard.  
Recognizing our plight, he fearlessly came  
To these horrible countries so forgetful of God  
And tirelessly labored to dispel the illusion;  
Recalling his wonders we’re helplessly awed.  
And in one small section of all the creation,  
He helped some of us to the spiritual path;  
Revealed God as a person. For our reclamation  
Placed us at the feet of Kṣīracora Gopīnātha.  
Prabhupāda, you are our eternal father  
For, more than supplying us our room and board,  
You’ve relieved us all of this saṁsāra bother  
And allowed us to live in the dhāma of the Lord.  
Exchanging old habits for spiritual programs,  
You’ve given us all a new life to live.  
And beyond simply living, we now have a mission:  
To all other souls the good fortune to give.  
O dear Prabhupāda, you’ve great hopes for this movement  
This branch as it is, of Lord Gaurāṅga’s tree.  
Oh, help us cooperate in your “absence,”  
Discovering strength that lies in unity.  
Our debt is infinite, its nature divine.  
Our blissful position is to try to serve  
Your lotus feet till past the end of time,  
And from this firm purpose may we never swerve.  
Like you, we have great hopes for Gopīnātha’s New Remuṇā.  
We hope by our efforts to give you some pleasure,  
Endeavoring to achieve your purpose in this area,  
Although our attempts may be too small to measure.  
We hope that this temple can be a strong limb  
Of the body of ISKCON, to which you gave birth.  
And carrying your spiritual power and courage,  
We’ll make up a force that can change all the earth.  
And so, Gurudeva, on this auspicious day,  
We thank you and pray for your mercy so dear,  
Full knowing that as long as we try to please you,  
You’re verily living within all of us, here.

*Your eternal servants in Toronto ISKCON, New Remuṇā Dhāma,*

## Trivandrum

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracārine*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

All glories, all glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda

On this most auspicious blessed day of all eternal time. There is nothing greater in all the fourteen worlds than your sacred appearance. In the invocation of Śrī Īśopaniṣad it is stated, oṁ pūrṇam adaḥ pūrṇam idaṁ pūrṇāt pūrṇam udacyate. This *mantra* means that whatever emanates from the Complete Whole or Absolute Truth remains perfect and complete. But without the presence of the purest spiritual master—you, Śrīla Prabhupāda—this world remains incomplete.

The masses of the world go on struggling like blind rats, simply interested in everything but Kṛṣṇa, *guru* and his servants. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, taught everyone just the opposite—love Kṛṣṇa, *guru* and his servants and nothing else. Materialistic persons keep to themselves what they cherish most; but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give the people, as Lord Caitanya did, what you cherish most, *kṛṣṇa-sevā* and *kṛṣṇa-prema.* You taught the highest standard of God realization to people who were factually the most unqualified living entities, and you changed their hearts from lust, greed and anger to pure unalloyed love for Kṛṣṇa. Only the most empowered and confidential servant of Lord Caitanya could accomplish this. Even other great Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas* and saints have not accomplished what you have done in such a short time. But the world is slowly turning to *sanātana-dharma*, by the sincere army of true Vaiṣṇavas that you have made and trained. All over the world people are now beginning to realize the universe’s greatest spiritual leader—a leader of complete and perfect love and compassion. We cannot rest until we see that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are properly glorified as the greatest *ācārya* of all time. To our very last breath we will preach your glories and message. At last Kṛṣṇa’s true words will pervade and guide the entire universe. But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have to admit that we are your little children and inexperienced in many ways. Therefore, on this most auspicious day, we beg for your causeless guidance, mercy, inspiration and compassion upon us, who have no other loved one than you, our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda. Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!

*Your eternally indebted servants,*

Gaurāṅga dāsa / Nṛsiṁha dāsa / Śyāmasundara dāsa / Gokulacandra dāsa / Śāstivara dāsa / Bāla Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Śyāmasundara dāsa / Kṛṣṇāveśa-devī dāsī

## Veracruz

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracārine*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The most tragic sequence of events in the material world repeats itself: the Lord’s illusory energy again inflicts defeat upon the living entities in the form of vain efforts, vague plans, unconscious desires.

The lesson is harsh—with Kṛṣṇa one does not compete!

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our eternal savior!

The age of Kali has dashed to pieces our uncertain hopes, and victors we insist we are, even in the shadow of defeat.

So remote and distant ftom the all-perfect, eternal enjoyer.

Fulfilling their promise of destruction, the sinful waters of conditioned life press in around us.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our eternal savior!

O Kṛṣṇa! How long have we awaited him?

He is Your most beloved friend and servant, and our eternal master.

By his unlimited mercy he has dissipated the dark clouds of sin from our hearts, and in their place he has etched the radiant vision of a beautiful world we somehow knew existed.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our eternal savior!

Who of us will have the great fortune to take into our hearts the dust of your lotus feet, the treading of which put an end to the cycle of birth and death?

When will the day come when by our intense preaching and devotional chanting your movement will be received with tumultuous exultation everywhere?

The imposing presence of your vānī has broken the discordant silence of our minds.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our eternal savior!

Being widely distributed, your monumental books have become a veritable repository for the faith and hope of all mankind.

The powerful influence of māyā, which keeps the poor souls of this earth in illusion, is crushed under the relentless force of your immortal bala.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our eternal savior!

O divine master, servant of Gaurāṅga!

We beg of you to intercede on our behalf at the end of this life’s struggle. With you there, the battle’s end will not be one of agony.

We shall emerge victorious, O light of Gaurāṅga!

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our eternal savior!

Your glorious works quickly nullify all deficiencies in man; the magical illusion created by māyā ceases its artificial reality.

It is your ISKCON which is our real source of strength and your love which has saved the world from imminent destruction.

You are the victor!

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our eternal savior!

Please accept this humble homage on this glorious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

Your eternal servants from Śrī-dhāma Veracruz, Mahā-saṅkīrtana Mandira

## Vienna

Let the sharp words of the critics pour on our heads; we don’t mind.  
Let the politicians call us crazy and mad. That’s all right.  
Let our brothers and sisters say we are frustrated and fanatic, that’s their right.  
Let the philosophers say we’re proud, and let the religionists say we’re unfit and unqualified; it’s their duty.  
But nevertheless, our determination to always remember, serve and worship the lotus feet of Śrī Gurudeva, His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, will not move an inch.

Life after life, birth after birth,  
We wish to be his humble servants.

Jaya Gurudeva dāsa brahmacārī / Ānanda-vardhana dāsa brahmacārī / Agnideva dāsa brahmacārī / Vivasvān dāsa brahmacārī / Bharata dāsa adhikārī / Āśā-siddhi-devī dāsī / Bhakta Hans / Bhaktin Gaby

## Vṛndāvana

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Back in 1967 you envisioned an American House in Vṛndāvana as you gazed out across the courtyard of Rādhā-Dāmodara at the sacred *samādhi* of Rūpa Gosvāmī. Two years earlier you had left Brajabhumi and your Lords Śrī Govinda, Gopīnātha, and Dāmodara on the order of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You had sailed to America, that remote corner of the world with its millions of automobiles blindly whizzing on superhighways, and supermarkets with the sound of music (heard even here). We were amongst the thousands of youthful dropouts rejecting our fathers’ wealth, loitering on the street, when you found us. You taught us a new song in a strange land, giving us hope, and a hymn which some called the hippie anthem and you called the *mahā-mantra,* Hare Kṛṣṇa. You showed us how to dance to the tune of a different drummer, and we danced. How we danced! And we will continue to dance to your tune if we hear you and listen to you and follow your instructions to the “t,” and not fall down. By your grace we will dance with you back to Godhead, past the *karmīs,* the Māyāvādīs and the mundane wranglers.

Out of divine love you had given your heart to your fledgling disciples and would-be disciples and even those crazies who rejected you. You had such a big heart it almost broke. So you returned to Vṛndāvana to recuperate in the fire of its balming heat. The things that disturbed Kīrtanānanda and Acyutānanda, you found bracing, and the flies you scarcely noticed at all. But most surprising was that even in Vṛndāvana there was no one like you. Not the other *sādhus,* the *bābājīs,* the *kavirājas,* nor even your Godbrothers. No one else was so attractive to the heart. No one else was so able to penetrate the falsity. No one else so wise. No one else so loving. No one else so attached to Kṛṣṇa. No one else so regal and yet so simple. No one else so compassionate to leave Vṛndāvana to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the West. Now we see that Vṛndāvana is our real home, not Broadway or Los Angeles, not Portland or Oakland or England. Will there always be an England? No. But they are, by your grace, meant to be outposts of Vṛndāvana through the temples you have established there like so many embassies of the spiritual world.

The outposts all established, your American House has been built, only it is no longer American, but international. As was prophesied, you built a house big enough for all the world to take shelter of. Now let your *samādhi* monument arise on the sands of Raman Reti and tower over the babel of confusing sectarianism. Let us proclaim your greatness and ring your clarion message to every corner of the globe. As the earliest devotees discovered, it was not the many faces of Hinduism or India that sustained us, but you. You, alone and dauntless, ventured across the seas on the *Jaladuta* and brought to us Vṛndāvana. No one else has been so munificent in distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness. No one was like you then. No one is like you now, and no one will ever be. You alone are the lord of our heart.

*The residents of ISKCON, Vṛndāvana*

## Washington, D.C.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept these 108 lines of poetry, which we humbly offer in the dust of your lotus feet.

In thoughtfulness I try to write, Your picture’s on the wall.  
What can I say to praise you, lord, The kindest of them all?  
From Vṛndāvana to Lower East Side You came without a friend.  
Who could have known how many lives Your love-filled words would mend?  
Bhaktivinoda and Gaurakiśora And your dear Gurudeva  
Each in his way prepared for you, From them you came, to save.  
We could not know how great you were, We know we never will;  
Your glories expanding on and on, Our value almost nil.  
Completely lost, we fallen souls, No chance had we, no hope,  
Till you, beloved Prabhupāda, Cast us your loving rope.  
We did not know how lost we were, We felt ourselves secure.  
In māyā’s lap we’d laid our heads, Our future doom was sure.  
You did not mind the filthiness, Though others shied away.  
By Kṛṣṇa’s grace you were untouched, Like a lotus flower in May.  
You taught us how to worship God, Reluctant though we were.  
And when despair would cloud our minds, You’d cause our hearts to stir:  
“Chant sixteen rounds of Kṛṣṇa’s names And from four sins refrain.  
No power within this mortal world Will make you fall again!”  
Each loving word you spoke gave strength To keep us moving on.  
You left your path for us to tread And go to where you’ve gone.  
You, loving father, gave us all The gifts a child should have.  
Not temporary gifts of dust; Eternal gifts like salve—  
To soothe the burning pain of lust And friends—anger and greed—  
To cure our cataracted eyes From blindness to be freed.  
You gave us Kṛṣṇa in His form Of marble to be seen  
By we conditioned souls, whose eyes Can’t see beyond the screen.  
A motley band, you gave us all A mission to fulfill;  
Remembrance of your guiding words Gives hope of victory still.  
We owe you all—our life and soul—You pulled us from the well.  
Misled by blinded fools we sat In suffering there to dwell.  
Though cynical and hard of heart, Your love evoked our tears,  
The price to pay for kṛṣṇa-prema Subsiding all our fears.  
And still we cry to hear your voice Producing nectar sweet  
To drench our minds and hearts and ears, Refreshed from māyā’s heat.  
O miracle-worker, Prabhupāda! Who can compare with you?  
Made monkeys dance with peacock steps, Gave dead men life anew.

And though you’ve traveled on somewhere, You’ve left us everything. Though māyā lures, we shall not budge From ‘neath your sheltering wing.  
What need have we to go elsewhere To search for something more? The path you gave is sure and safe And straight to Kṛṣṇa’s door.

We saw your style—that’s our goal. We want to be like you.  
These rascals, scientists and fools—“Kick on the face with shoe!”  
O Prabhupāda! You’re still with us. What need have we to fear?  
You’ve left your books for us to read, Now everything is clear.  
We yearn to bask in your presence And now by your kind will  
*Līlāmṛta* and ITV Reveal your darśana still.  
And those with purest hearts can see Your lotus face all ‘round,  
Kind smiling eyes to reassure Your blessings still abound.  
And we, like children, babes in woods, Now tested from all sides,  
Hold tightly to your lotus hand, Fearless of Kali’s tides.  
We vow to make your mission ours; Our lives to you we give.  
What value have we otherwise? What need have we to live?  
Though we may yearn to leave this place Our time is not yet due.  
We’ll stay and fight till glorious death And then come back to you.

Begging to remain,  
Your humble servants at  
New Hastināpura Dhāma,

## Back to Godhead

*Cakṣu-dāna dila ye, janme janme prabhu sei*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances, eternally, at your lotus feet.

In a verse you quote in *The Nectar of Devotion,* Hanumān says, “My dear Lord, if You like You can give me salvation from this material existence, or the privilege of merging into Your existence, but I do not wish any of these things. I do not want anything which diminishes my relationship with You as master and servant, even after liberation.”

We want to follow in the footsteps of Hanumān in our mood of serving you. We want only to please you by our service. We don’t want to be merchants with you—“If you’ll give me ecstasy, I’ll do this for you; if you’ll give me knowledge, I’ll do that for you; if you give me liberation, I’ll do something else for you.” We think there’s nothing better, nothing more valuable or precious, than the privilege of serving your lotus feet. *Yaṁ labdhvā cāparaṁ lābhaṁ manyate nādhikaṁ tataḥ.* When one actually appreciates the tasty nectar of service to your lotus feet, he thinks, “This is the best thing. Nothing could be better than this. I just want to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda.” *Yasmin sthito na duḥkhena guruṇāpi vicālyate:* “Even if so many difficulties come upon me, I just want to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus feet.”

As you once said, *“Feet* means person.” We want to serve you, personally. When we surrender to you, we do not surrender merely to a functionary or a vague, impersonal *tattva.* We surrender to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with all your special personal qualities, all the characteristics that make you different from everybody else.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is you who have given the mercy of Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya to us and to the entire modern world, and it is you we want to keep always at the center of our Society. It is your Society, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and it is your name we want to glorify throughout the world on the ISKCON banner. It is your instructions we want to take to heart and make the center of our lives. You are the one who followed your spiritual master’s desire, who risked everything to come west and save us from material existence. (Everyone else stayed home in India, or else went out and came back defeated.) You are the one who is publishing books and distributing them all over the world. You are the one who is distributing the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* and Lord Caitanya’s gift of the *saṅkīrtana* movement. You are the one who has built a house of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to give shelter to the whole world. Why should we surrender to anyone apart from you?

*bhavantam evānucaran nirantaraḥ*  
*praśānta-niḥśeṣa-manorathāntaraḥ*  
*kadāham aikāntika-nitya-kiṅkaraḥ*  
*praharṣayiṣyāmi sanātha-jīvitam*

Only by constantly serving your lotus feet can we feel actual peace of mind and freedom from material desires. We pray to engage only as eternal servants exclusively dedicated to you. Our life has meaning only when we devote ourselves to you, our perfect master. Only then can we feel completely joyful.

If you are pleased by our service, then Kṛṣṇa is pleased, and that can be our only true pleasure. And if we displease you, we are useless.

We want to follow in the footsteps of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s son Pradyumna: “Pradyumna’s attachment for Kṛṣṇa was always exhibited by his action. Whenever he was ordered by his father to execute something, he would immediately execute the order, taking the task as nectarean even though it may have been poison. Similarly, whenever he would find something to be disapproved of by his father, he would immediately reject it as poison, even though it may have been nectarean.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we place our heads at your lotus feet, desiring always to carry out your orders and satisfy you. The only nectar we want to taste is the nectar of unalloyed and ever-increasing devotional service to your lotus feet.

*You servants at* Back to Godhead *magazine, Philadelphia,*

Jayādvaita Swami / Draviḍa dāsa / Jaya Jagadīśa dāsa / Yamarāja dāsa / Tattva-vit dāsa / Bali Mahārāja dāsa / Śāstra dāsa / Madhyamā-devī dāsī

## Bala Books

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. You nurtured us, your disciples, as a parent would a child, with the milk of kindness, watched us come of age and then sent us out to carry on your mission. How clumsy we are, how much our efforts lack the finesse of spiritual realization that your life so gloriously personified.

Yet we will persevere, despite our clumsiness, for you have also taught us that Vaiṣṇavas only live to impart Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others. In Rome, you told us that you left Vṛndāvana to preach in the West, and when we said that we didn’t think you ever really left Vṛndāvana, you replied, “That’s a fact. Why should I be selfish? I brought Vṛndāvana with me to give to you.”

We wish to follow your example of selfless devotion. On this auspicious occasion celebrating your appearance we humbly submit this collective offering as tiny souls mercifully engaged by you in a service of great importance. “What is learned early in life,” you once told us, “is never lost,” and we know from your own example how as a child you were absorbed in serving your Deities, enacting plays about Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes, and hearing His glories from books such as *Rāmāyaṇa* and *Mahābādrata.* We, your servants, are attempting to give the millions of conditioned souls in children’s bodies the opportunity to similarly learn about the glories of Lord Kṛṣṇa through the publications you have encouraged us to produce. By your mercy, and with the blessings of your senior disciples, this work is meeting with great success.

If by our small efforts the young people of this age can learn about Kṛṣṇa consciousness, then your mission will become even more recognized, and our lives will have had true meaning.

*Your servants in the Bala Books project,*

Yogeśvara dāsa / Caturātmā dāsa / Ādya dāsa / Ārādhana dāsa / Prahlāda-priya dāsa / Jyotirmayī-devī dāsī / Rādhā-Madhana-mohana-devī dāsī / Pūrṇaśakti-devī dāsī / Bob Golden

## Bhaktivedanta Institute India

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracārine*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya*  
*jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena*  
*tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ*  
*paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande*  
*śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda! Please kindly accept our prostrated obeisances to your sacred lotus feet.

Since your departure from this physical world our hearts are increasingly burning in separation from Your Divine Grace. We are like small boys playing in the middle of this material forest without being properly conscious of the imminent danger in which the illusory energy places us conditioned souls every moment. But your magnanimity is so indescribable that Your Divine Grace is always ransoming us from the dangerous situations. You are the perfect mystic. Through your mercy everything is revealed possible.

Now we are growing in your separation, and we need to prove to you the spiritual maturity of our understanding of your mission, the unique mission of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu entrusted to you as His perfect pure devotee. We have the responsibility to present the teachings of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the intelligent class of men. Your Divine Grace created the Bhaktivedanta Institute for this purpose. Only you really understood the significant message of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and only you can fully occupy the center of the inner chamber of our hearts, because you are our unique lord, Guru Mahārāja and beloved father. Nobody else can take this most significant position in our lives. We are all made of your mercy, and as we are neophyte souls, there is no possibility for us to understand the intricate mellows of devotional service without being blessed by the dust of your lotus feet and the feet of those advanced Vaiṣṇavas who in your absence are responsible to fulfill the success of your mission.

A flood of transcendental ecstasy covered the earth when you were present among us. Now, in your absence, we are confronting difficulties and pains, and we are calling you: Prabhupāda! Prabhupāda! What to do? Prabhupāda! Prabhupāda!...

You left the transcendental realm of Śrī Vṛndāvana—where you always are—to accept ourselves as your disciples. Out of unlimited compassion for the universe, you instructed how we can be elevated to the transcendental position. You are the witness, prince and emissary of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Gopīnāthajī, and time has come knocking at our doors, clamoring for a real attitude of surrender. We need to show at this time that we are capable of cooperating as you want in the best spirit of purity and love to fulfill your desires. We are bound to surrender completely to your divine instructions, throwing far away our material motivations and personal desires, our doubts and suspicions, which are like demoniac *anarthas* obstructing our pure love for you and Lord Gaurāṅga.

Here in India you are our eternal companion. You instructed Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami to spread the message of *sanātana-dharma* to the intelligent class of mankind through your unique vision of the Bhaktivedanta Institute. This high Vaiṣṇava personality is like a swan who carries the pure fragrance of your message without fear, instead of undesirable and unpredictable circumstances. We all have in ISKCON great faith in him, because he is perfectly attached to your lotus feet and free of misconceptions. We pray with vehemence to you for the blessings of all your beloved devotees in order to better serve your instructions more and more with undeviated and undisturbed faith. We pray to you, our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, to live always with you, forever serving your *Bhāgavatam* purports, enhancing more and more your unlimited glories for the benefit of all suffering *jīvas.*

Your *līlāmṛta,* your divine pastimes, are living with us in the fire of loving remembrance. We are devoid of devotion and intelligence. Please accept us fallen souls as your eternal servants. Wherever you are playing with Lord Kṛṣṇa now, please accept this humble offering to your divine lotus feet on this blessed event of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

*Your servants,*

Bhaktiśāstrī Goswami / Viśuddha-sattva dāsa brahmacārī / Ānandamaya dāsa brahmacārī / Mahamān dāsa adhikārī / Śaṅkhabhṛt dāsa adhikārī

## Bhaktivedanta Institute San Francisco

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Dear Jagad-guru, by your mercy our memory of you is still firmly planted in our hearts, and it is guiding us through the tests you predicted would come. By your mercy the spiritual intelligence that you gave us is still active and discriminating against all deviations from the service of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. By your mercy, our subtle bodies are occasionally fully purified by a glimpse of you in our dreams. By your mercy, our association is still the association of your disciples, all of whom have individual and unique realizations of your mercy. By your mercy, the necessity for a lifetime of strict *sāddhana* is becoming an unshakeable conviction. By your mercy, the need to respect our Godbrothers’ commitment to you, and to cooperate with each other to achieve our common goals of satisfying you, has become a priority.

By your mercy, we at the Bhaktivedanta Institute are committing ourselves to fulfilling one of your most cherished goals: to create an intelligent, brahminical class of men who can preach by example to the intelligent section of society. This is no easy task, and it can only be done through the agency of your mercy. But since you have exhibited your mercy to us in so many ways already, we are convinced that you can remove the defects and doubts still existing within our hearts, so that we may carry your mercy, as insignificant peons, to the intelligent and responsible class in society. This is our only hope to earn the right of eternally keeping this mercy you’ve so causelessly given to us.

*Your inarticulate beggars,*  
Bhaktivedanta Institute

## International Ministry of Public Affairs

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracārine*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“Some look on the soul as amazing, some describe him as amazing, and some hear of him as amazing, while others, even after hearing about him, cannot understand him at all.” (Bg. 2.29)**

The activities of the pure devotee are truly amazing; they can never be restricted or limited by the laws of material nature. Śrīla Prabhupāda—after coming to America at an advanced age, you, in addition to performing a multitude of other transcendental feats, wrote volume after volume of books to enlighten this darkened world in the age of Kali with knowledge of the soul and Kṛṣṇa. The amazing quality of these books is that just by hearing them, anyone anywhere can immediately relish your association and the association of Kṛṣṇa.

You explain in the *Bhagavad-gītā* the rare fortune of a person who comes in contact with such pure association. “It is very difficult to find a man who perfectly understands the position of the soul, the Supersoul ... and all other major and minor details. And it is still more difficult to find a man who has actually derived full benefit from knowledge of the soul, and who is able to describe the position of the soul in different aspects.” Your transcendental books describe the “position of the soul in different aspects”; they are the basis of our preaching, and they are the support and guide by which we advance towards pure love of Kṛṣṇa.

It is our humble prayer that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your infinite compassion, will be pleased with our meager efforts to attract the minds of the conditioned souls towards the ocean of nectar contained in these transcendental literatures.

We pray that by accepting our imperfect service, Gurudeva, you will allow us to take part in spreading your unlimited glories throughout the three worlds.

*Your eternal servants all over the world,*

Mukunda Goswami / Bhutātmā dāsa (Los Angeles) / Mahārudra dāsa (Los Angeles) / Uddhava dāsa (Los Angeles) / Drutakarmā (Los Angeles) / Hṛṣīkeśānanda dāsa (Laguna Beach) / Locana dāsa (Los Angeles) / Kṛṣṇa-upadeśa dāsa (Los Angeles) / Rakṣaṇa dāsa (Los Angeles) / Maitreya Muni dāsa (Los Angeles) / Bhūśaya dāsa (Detroit) / Premārṇava dāsa (Detroit) / Toṣaṇa Kṛṣṇa dāsa (New York) / Kṛṣṇa Gopāla dāsa (Washington, DC) / Rāghava dāsa (Hawaii) / Mahābuddhi dāsa (New Vrindaban) / Śubhānanda dāsa (Philadelphia) / Kalki dāsa (Chicago) / Sunanda dāsa (Dallas) / Prabhukṛpa dāsa (Denver) / Kuṇḍalī dāsa (Baltimore) / Devī-deva dāsa (Toronto) / Vegavān dāsa (Sweden) / Hari Kṛṣṇa dāsa (Sweden) / Indriya-damana dāsa (France) / Gaura Kṛṣṇa dāsa (Italy) / Kṛṣṇa Caitanya dāsa (Italy) / Guru-Gaurāṅga dāsa (Switzerland) / Gokulānanda dāsa (England) / Mādhavendra Purī dāsa (England) / Dhanañjaya dāsa (Vṛndāvana) / Pañcaratna dāsa (Māyāpur) / Mahāprabhu dāsa (Bombay) / Dāmodara Paṇḍita dāsa (Bombay) / Jagat-puruṣa dāsa (Bombay) / Citta-hārī dāsa (Australia) / Gaura-Gopāla dāsa (Australia) / Bhakta Scott Wolfe (Los Angeles) / Bhakta Bill Bowmer (Los Angeles) / Bhakta Jeff Rubin (Los Angeles) / Yaśodāmāi-devī dāsī (Los Angeles ) / Nandinī-devī dāsī (Los Angeles) / Gaṅgā-devī dāsī (Laguna Beach) / Gītāmṛta-devī dāsī (Italy) / Draupadī-devī dāsī (England) / Bhaktin Charlotte Johnson (Los Angeles) / Bhaktin Cheryl Del Rosso (Los Angeles)

## Midwest U.S.A. College TSKP

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracārine*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**In Praise of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Descended by Kṛṣṇa’s will from the spiritual world.  
Destined—by him spiritual knowledge is unfurled.  
Lord Caitanya’s most empowered associate.  
Intimate servant—His anxiety to alleviate.  
“How will all the fallen jīvas be delivered?”  
Since time immemorial—connection severed.  
Desperate, submerged in the material ocean,  
Māyā’s wave upon wave—all hope broken.  
Gasping and struggling, swimming was no solution,  
Visions of dry land—only illusion.  
Just then, on the horizon, a ship made of jñāna.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda the captain, the fuel—compassion.  
Guiding the vessel, untouched by the sea,  
Amidst the wretched cries: “Have mercy on me!”  
He tossed the life saver—Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra.  
“Here, take this pakora”—prasāda the diet.  
“Nitya-siddha-kṛṣṇa-prema ‘sādhya’ kabhu naya  
śravaṇādi-śuddha-citte karaye udaya”  
“Go on hearing and chanting and engaging the senses.”  
Seawater too bitter, but this nectar quenches!

The process sublime, Effect in no time. Unfathomable ocean, Like miraculous potion, Shrinking away Day after day, Until a calf’s hoofprint contains All the water that remains.

And so, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, As you have saved us from the dangerous sea, Shown us Kṛṣṇa’s separation is just māyā’s dream, We humbly request You to kindly invest Us with a small fraction Of your compassion And help us traverse That jeopardous course Once more for the glory of your majesty, Spreading the mahā-mantra, Cooking prasād, Distributing your books—“Kṛṣṇa is God!”

You’ve said there is no better way to repay “All that you do, all that you think, all that you say, Devote your life to further my mission.” Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, grant us that vision. Give our lives worth, Make us your slaves, No care for being saved Our only intent—To be your instrument.

*Your worthless servants,*

Midwest U.S.A. College TSKP,

Gaṇapati dāsa Swami / Mitrasena dāsa brahmacārī / Bhuvaneśvara dāsa brahmacārī

## Prabhupāda-Līlāmṛta Staff

In L.A. we saw you at first, Glowing forceful, speaking so true.  
Reaching out to give you a gift, Flower taken you said, “Thank you.”  
Your preaching attracted our minds To strive for spiritual life  
Beyond the limits of matter, Body, society and wife.

Prabhupāda, you spoke of Kṛṣṇa, The supreme person, our best friend,  
Who’ll take us back to Godhead When this body reaches the end.  
Your eyes would speak, your hands would, too, Destroying doubts with yoga’s sword,  
Slashing scientists’ sloppy guess With Vedic truth, absolute lord.

Morning walks, no one could catch you, Half your age or even young boys.  
Speeding along the park or beach, Never lost a breath, step or poise.  
Demigods and sages came To see you dancing at Bury Place.  
Known to Brahmā and Nārada Your name and your lotus face.

Janmāṣṭamī, in New Vrindaban,  
Hearing Kṛṣṇa book hour by hour  
You became happy and refreshed;  
Our heads fell lower and lower.

A total stranger you see, and Bringing smiles to his life of tears  
With charming wit, kind words and love, Still he remembers you for years.  
At the Manor you met Graham Hill, Speaking cars and racing when he  
Said high speeds remind him of God. You change gears, giving Kṛṣṇa free.

Devotees suggested you change To spirit George Harrison’s name  
“Why? He is already son of Hari. Just let him keep the same.”  
Psychologists came to see you In Caracas to prove you wrong.  
You stunned them with intelligence, Exposing their minds’ babbling song.

The Bombay struggle at Juhu Devotees could barely endure.  
Alone you fought, a temple to build To worship your Lord for sure.  
With these pastimes and all others Everything that you tried to do  
Succeeded as you constantly Attempted to please Kṛṣṇa anew.

Now your darśana is found in tapes, Feelingful bhajanas, nectar-sweet  
Bhāgavatam, Gītā, Queen Kuntī Cane-clicking walks, your lotus feet.  
You gave us I. T. V. to spread Kṛṣṇa’s fame through the world enmasse,  
But we can’t stop watching “Ācārya,” Awed by your devotional class.

Transcendental books, a lifeline Extended from your lotus heart,  
Pull us out of māyā’s ocean Keeping us close, never apart.  
You invited Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa For us to worship, love and serve.  
May we never think of Them as burden Or from your orders swerve.

Thirteen times around the world you flew, Building temples one hundred and eight.  
Sixty million books, five thousand disciples—Who could be so great?  
Every word a song, step a dance, You showed us the spiritual sky.  
​​​​​​​Karatālas, mṛdaṇga, whompers, too, The swami step, hands reaching high.

Gaura-pūrṇimā and Ratha-yātrā, Big festivals praising the Lord.  
You left us Jagannātha’s cart To free anyone who pulls the cord.  
Missing your personal darśana We seek guidance from your letters,  
​​​​​​​Chastising and encouraging, Freeing us from māyā’s fetters.

Great strength in this association, Worldwide ISKCON society,  
We needn’t stray nor go away From your feet to find variety.  
In Vṛndāvana you have come to rest At Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma’s lotus feet,  
Your example so pure and daring, “Give Kṛṣṇa to everyone you meet.”

Our debt to you is eternal “Print books, distribute books and preach.”  
The whole world is waiting for us To set the example and teach.  
In sound, form, pastimes, ever fresh, Prabhupāda’s not gone but living,  
Growing in our hearts day by day, Always guiding, always giving.

As you once walked into our lives, Saved us, fed us, by your hand led  
Away from māyā’s illusion, Taking us home, back to Godhead.

Again, millions will have a chance  
To meet Lord Kṛṣṇa’s dearmost friend  
By reading the Līlāmṛta.  
All their miseries you will end.

*Your servants on the biography staff,*

Maṇḍaleśvara dāsa / Nāgarāja dāsa / Mahā-nidhi dāsa / Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa dāsa / Gaura-pūrṇimā dāsa / Haladhāra dāsa / Vimala-devī dāsī / Rukmiṇī-devī dāsī / Aṣṭa-sakhī-devī dāsī / Ācārya-devī dāsī
